bis  l  annual report 2018-19  i 5 
4   i bis  l  annual report 2018-19
Bureau of Indian Standards (BIS), the National Standards Body of India, was 
established under the BIS Act 1986, assuming the functions of the erstwhile 
Indian Standards Institution (ISI). BIS is responsible for the harmonious 
development of the activities of standardization, marking and quality 
certification of goods and for matters connected therewith or incidental thereto. 
With the integration of global economies, countries around the world 
are strategically positioning themselves at an international level to gain 
a competitive edge and achieve economic benefits. As standards and 
conformity assessment are significantly influencing global trade, countries 
are adopting strategies to seek positions in international standardization 
fora to influence international standards in their favour. India, in tandem 
with national standardization strategies, is interacting with various industry 
associations to harness Indian skills and strengthen and review the existing 
standards and make forays into new avenues relevant from the view point of 
standardization. 
Standardization supports economic growth, enhances competitiveness and 
fosters technological developments. It is also a tool to influence public policies 
such as those on health and safety, protection of environment and consumer 
protection. By ensuring a level playing field and defining common criteria of 
acceptance of goods, standards are increasingly being used as tools to support 
regulatory framework and to facilitate trade. In this regard, BIS is interacting 
with stakeholders to bring a number of products under mandatory certification 
thereby protecting the consumer's interest.
BIS, through its core activities of standardization and conformity 
assessment, has been benefiting the national economy by providing safe, 
reliable and quality goods; minimizing health hazards to consumers; 
protecting the environment, promoting exports and imports substitutes; 
controlling proliferation of varieties, etc. The standards and certification 
scheme of BIS, apart from benefiting the consumers and industries, also 
supports various public policies, especially in areas of product safety, 
consumer protection, food safety, environment protection, building and 
construction, etc. 
During the year, BIS has specifically been addressing various national 
priorities and other Government initiatives in the area of smart cities 
infrastructure and renewable energy infrastructure through solar energy 
standards and technical textiles to conserve natural resources. In standards 
development, BIS continued to address issues of technology changes and 
advancements, climate change, environment and energy conservation, health 
and safety conditions and facilitation of trade. In the area of conformity 
assessment, BIS worked towards making the processes simpler and faster.

the customer's needs for quality

bis has its Headquarters at New Delhi. It has 
five regional offices (ROs) located at Kolkata 
(Eastern), Chennai (Southern), Mumbai 
(Western), Chandigarh (Northern) and Delhi 
(Central). Under the Regional Offices are the 
Branch Offices (BOs). There are 32 BOs located 
at 28 different locations namely Ahmedabad, 
Bengaluru, Bhubaneswar, Bhopal, Chandigarh, 
Chennai, Coimbatore, Dehradun, Delhi, Faridabad, 
Ghaziabad, Guwahati, Hubli, Hyderabad, Jaipur, 
Jammu, Jamshedpur, Kochi, Kolkata, Lucknow, 
Mumbai, Nagpur, Parwanoo, Patna, Pune, Raipur, 
Rajkot and Vishakhapatnam. The BOs serve as 
an effective link between State Governments, 
industries, technical institutions, consumer 
organizations, etc., of the region. In order to cater 
to the needs of North Karnataka, a BIS Branch 
office was opened at Hubli during 2018-19. 
To cater to the testing needs of certification 
activity, BIS has eight laboratories in the 
country namely Central Laboratory (CL), 
Sahibabad; Western Regional Office Laboratory 
(WROL), Mumbai; Northern Regional Office 
Laboratory (NROL), Mohali; Eastern Regional 
Office Laboratory (EROL), Kolkata; Southern 
Regional Office Laboratory (SROL), Chennai; 
Bengaluru Branch Office Laboratory (BNBOL), 
Bengaluru; Patna Branch Office Laboratory 
(PBOL), Patna; Guwahati Branch Office 
Laboratory (GBOL), Guwahati.

the Governing Council and the executive Committee provide the policy directives

Governing Council  
as per the provisions of the BIS act, 2016, the Bureau shall be a body corporate. the 
members of the Governing Council constitute the Bureau. the Governing Council 
consists of 27 members representing the Members of parliament, the Central and the 
State governments, industry, scientific and research institutions, consumer organizations 
and professional bodies, regulatory bodies, national accreditation Board, with the union

Minister of Consumer affairs, Food and public Distribution as its president and the 
Minister of State for Consumer affairs, Food and public Distribution as its Vice-president.
the first Governing Council meeting was held on november 22, 2017, subsequently 
the second Governing Council meeting was held on December 20, 2018.  
 
Executive Committee
the executive Committee consists of the Director General as ex-officio Chairman and 11 
other members appointed with the approval of the Central Government. the executive 
Committee performs, exercises and discharges such functions, powers and duties of the 
Bureau as delegated by the Governing Council. 
Five meetings of executive Committee were held on april 5, 2018, June 5, 2018, 
July 27, 2018, September 5, 2018 and February 5, 2019.

BIS has been providing tangible benefits to the national economy  
by providing safe and reliable quality goods, minimizing health  
hazards to consumers, promoting exports and imports substitute  
and controlling over-proliferation of varieties through standardization, 
certification and testing.
OuR pERfORMANCE
AS the National Standards Body, BIS 
develops Indian Standards through a 
consultative mechanism in technical 
committees comprising stakeholders so 
that views and interests of all are given due 
consideration while formulating a standard. 
The stakeholders can broadly be categorized 
as industry, consumers/users, technologists 
(R&D and scientific institutions, academia, 
etc.) and government departments/
regulators. A Standard by definition is 
a voluntary document. The process of 
standards development follows the best 
international practices, which are based 
on principles of openness, transparency, 
impartiality and are consensus based. Apart 
from consultation within the technical 
committees, draft standards are also open 
for public views/comments. 
BIS also represents India in international 
standards bodies like International Organization 
for Standardization (ISO) and International 
Electrotechnical Commission (IEC), and 
participates actively in the international 
standardization work undertaken in these 
bodies. BIS presents the national viewpoint on 
various draft international standards during 
the process of development of these standards 
so that the country’s interest is protected and 
reflected in these standards. This also enables 
the BIS technical committees to consider 
adoption of the International Standards as 
Indian Standards with or without modifications. 
There are around 1,000 technical 
committees in BIS carrying out standardization 
work in 14 broad technological areas. During 
the year, 881 (new 484 and revised 397) 
standards were formulated. The total number 
of Indian Standards in force, as on March 31, 
2019, was 20,227. 
REVIEW Of STANDARDS
Indian Standards are reviewed as and when 
considered necessary, but at least once in five 
years to establish whether these standards are 
still relevant and to take appropriate action for 
reaffirmation, revision, issuing amendments, 
declare obsolescence or withdrawal. 
HARMONIZATION
In the present market scenario, India is facing 
the challenge of global competition. It is 
important to harmonize Indian Standards, as 
far as possible, with International Standards 
formulated by International Organization for 
Standardization (ISO) and the International 
Electrotechnical Commission (IEC). Further, 
India is a signatory to the WTO Agreement 
on Technical Barriers to Trade (TBT) 
whereby, member countries of WTO are 
required to align their National Standards 
with International Standards. However, 
country-specific concerns on health, safety, 
environment, national security and prevention 
of deceptive practices can be considered/ 
incorporated while formulating National 
Standards. BIS uses International Standards 
as a basis for standards development. 
During the year, 525 Indian Standards were 
harmonized with International Standards. 
A total of 5,812 Indian Standards have been 
harmonized with International Standards.
puBLICATION Of STANDARDS
Indian Standards are made available for use to 
the stakeholders after a gazette process and 
notification. During 2018-19, BIS published 
1,796 standards (980 new and 816 revised) 
and 154 amendments. The total number of 
standards in force as on 31 March, 2019

pRODuCT CERTIfICATION
BIS operates a Product Certification 
Scheme under the Bureau of Indian 
Standards Act, 2016 and the Rules  
thereunder and BIS (Conformity 
Assessment) Regulations, 2018. The 
presence of Standard Mark (popularly 
known as ISI mark) on a product indicates 
its conformity to the relevant Indian 
Standard. Before granting a licence to 
any manufacturer, BIS ascertains the 
availability of required infrastructure and 
capability of the manufacturer to produce 
and test the product conforming to the 
relevant Indian Standard. Samples drawn 
from the production line as well as from 
the market are tested in BIS laboratories/
recognized independent laboratories to 
ensure conformance of the product to 
the relevant Indian Standard. BIS also 
conducts surveillance visits to ensure 
conformance of licencees’ products to the 
During 2018-19, 4,082 new licences were granted, which 
include 18 products covered for the first time under 
the scheme. The total number of Indian Standards 
covered under BIS Certification Marks Scheme is 966. 
The total number of operative licences held by domestic 
manufacturers as on 31 March, 2019, was 34,414.

time licences for “glass feeding bottles” 
for which there is no licence in India as on 
date. This product is used by infants who 
are deprived of mother’s milk, and glass 
feeding bottles are a preferred choice 
compared to plastic feeding bottles, which 
are commonly used. For the first time, on 
11 January, 2019, Foreign Manufacturers 
Certification Department has conducted an 
'Orientation Programme for the Authorized 
Indian Representatives (AIR)' where 
presentations on requirements for obtaining 
BIS Certification for foreign manufacturers 
and the responsibilities of AIRs were made. 
COMpuLSORY REGISTRATION  
SCHEME (CRS) 
The Ministry of Electronics and Information 
Technology (Meity), then known as 
Department of Electronics & Information 
Technology (Deity), in consultation with 
BIS, notified ‘Electronics and information 
Technology Goods (Requirements for 
Compulsory Registration) Order, 2012’ 
on 3 October, 2012, mandating Compulsory 
Registration from BIS for 15 Electronics 
and Information Technology product 
categories based on its safety compliance 
to Indian Standards. 
A second Order was notified on 
13 November, 2014, bringing 15 more 
electronics and Information Technology 
products under the ambit of the scheme. 
Indian Language Support for Mobile Phones 
was mandated by Meity vide notification 
dated 24 October, 2016.
The scope of two existing products was 
expanded and 11 new product categories 
were added under the scheme by Meity vide 
notification dated 23 August, 2017. 
Ministry of New and Renewal Energy 
(MNRE) has also notified Solar Photovoltaic, 
Systems, Devices and Components Goods 
(Requirements for Compulsory Registration) 
Order, 2017 for five product categories 
through notification dated 5 September, 
2017. 
The Compulsory Registration 
Scheme is being operated by BIS.
The introduction of this Scheme is an 
alternative mechanism to the Compulsory 
Certification to facilitate growth of fast 
growing sectors like IT, and protect 
consumers from spurious and sub-standard 
products manufactured in India or abroad.

were carried out in 2018-19.

fOREIGN MANufACTuRERS 
CERTIfICATION SCHEME (fMCS)
BIS has been operating a separate scheme 
for foreign manufacturers since 2000. Under 
this scheme, foreign manufacturers can seek 
certification from BIS for use of BIS Standard 
Mark on their product(s). During 2018-19, 
136 licences have been issued under FMCS, 
taking the total number of operative licences 
to 874 for 100 Indian Standards. The 
licences granted cover various products such 
as steel & steel products, cement, tyres & 
tubes for automobile vehicles, plastic/glass 
feeding bottles, electric cables, switchgear 
products, plugs & socket outlets and 
switches, electrical energy meters, electrical 
appliances, motors, capacitors, HDPE & 
UPVC pipes, infant formula, caustic soda, 
compressors, float glass, mineral water, etc., 
in around 51 countries across the world. 
During the year, FMCS has granted first 
The Scheme envisages that no person 
shall manufacture, import, sell or distribute 
goods that do not conform to the Specified 
Standards and do not bear the Standard Mark 
along with the Registration number. 
The major products under the Scheme are:
  LED fixtures, lamps, drivers, flood 
lights, hand lamps, and lighting 
chains
  Recessed LED Luminaires, LED 
Luminaires for Road and Street 
Lighting, Luminaires for Emergency 
Lighting 
  Mobile phones, power banks for 
portable applications, smart watches
  Rechargeable cells/batteries
  UPS and invertors of capacity 10 kVA 
and below
  Microwave ovens
  Plasma/LCD/LED TVs/Visual display 
units/monitors
  Adapters for IT equipment, audio-
video products, and for household and 
similar electrical appliances
 Point-of-sale terminals, ADP machine
 Laptop/notebook/tablet
  Printers/plotters and scanners  
  Set-top box
  CCTV Cameras/CCTV Recorders
  USB driven Barcode readers, Barcode 
scanners, Iris scanners, Optical 
Fingerprint scanners
  Solar Photovoltaic Modules
The first Registration was granted by BIS 
on 12 June, 2013. As on 31 March, 2019, 
BIS has granted 17,251 registrations to 
manufacturers located in various countries 
including India.

Hallmarking of gold/silver jewellery/
artefact
Hallmarking of gold jewellery was 
started by BIS in April 2000 to provide 
third party assurance to consumers 
on the purity of gold jewellery or its 
fineness. The scheme for Hallmarking of 
silver jewellery/artefacts was launched 
in October 2005. Under the Scheme, 
while the jewellers are granted licence 
to sell hallmarked jewellery, Assaying 
& Hallmarking centres are recognized 
to assay the purity of the jewellery 
submitted by the licensed jeweller, along 
with declaration of purity and to apply 
hallmark on such jewellery which is 
found conforming to the relevant Indian 
Standard including declared fineness.
Hallmarking of Gold Bullion
Hallmarking of gold bullion in the fineness 
of 999 & 995 as per IS 1417: 2016 was 
started in October 2015. Under this 
scheme, a licence is granted to refineries/
mint that carry out refining of gold by 
electrolytic or aquaregia processes and 
have a laboratory with complete test 
facilities and accredited by NABL. So far, 
25 licences have been granted to refineries/ 
India Government Mint for gold bullion and 
coin as on 31 March, 2019.  
Gold Monetization Scheme
The Government of India has launched 
Gold Monetization Scheme on 5 
November, 2015. BIS has played an 
important role in the finalization 
and implementation of the Gold 
Monetization Scheme in association 
with the Department of Economic 
Affairs and Reserve Bank of India. Under 
the scheme, Assaying & Hallmarking 
Centres recognized by BIS have been 
qualified to act as Collection and Purity 
Testing Centres (CPTC). So far, 48 A&H 
centres and one jeweller have qualified 
to act as CPTC. The gold collected by 
CPTCs is to be refined by refineries 
licensed by BIS. 
promotion of Hallmarking 
To promote hallmarking in the country 
for effective consumer protection in gold 
jewellery trade, awareness programmes 
for jewellers are organized by BIS through 
its various Regional and Branch offices 
across the country. During the year, 57 
such jewellers’ awareness programmes 
were organized. Under the scheme 
for Hallmarking, during this period, for 
infrastructure building, assistance has been 
provided to one Assaying & Hallmarking 
centre in Silchar, Assam. Under the 
capacity building, 20 programmes have 
been organized for training of artisans, 
eight programmes have been organized for 
Assaying & Hallmarking personal training 
and four programmes for training of BIS 
officers were organized. 
In 2018-19, the number of hallmarking licences grew 
from 23,342 to 26,688. The BIS-recognised AHCs 
increased from 608 to 797, and the number of gold and 
silver articles hallmarked from 4.17 to 4.49 crore.

The Management Systems Certification 
Services are operated in accordance with ISO/
IEC 17021-1:2015 Conformity assessment—
Requirements for bodies providing audit 
and certification of management systems 
- Requirements. In the year 2018-19, four 
additional composite/hybrid type schemes 
have been launched, as follows: 
i) Ready Mixed Concrete Certification Scheme 
as per ISO 9001 and IS 4926;
ii) Road Traffic Safety Management Systems 
Certification Scheme as per IS/ISO 39001; 
iii) Integrated Milk Certification Scheme for 
Packaged Pasteurized Milk as per 13688 
along with ISO 22000;  
iv) Adventure Tourism Safety Management 
Systems as per IS/ISO 21101:2014. 
The Quality Management Systems (QMS) 
and Environmental Management Systems 
(EMS) Certification Schemes have been 
accredited by the National Accreditation 
Board for Certification Bodies (NABCB) and 
re-accreditation up to 15 March, 2021, for 
24 scope sectors (including 11 additional 
scope sectors) in QMS Certification and for 
five scope sectors (including three additional 
scope sectors) in EMS Certification.  
During the MSC Stakeholder’s Meet, BIS 
launched the module for online empanelment 
of auditors and online submission of 
applications on www.manakonline.in. An IT-
based basic online module for operation by 
Management Systems Coordination Officer 
(MSCO) has been developed with CDAC and 
implemented. Online module for receipt of 
application for grant of licence/renewal has 
been implemented. BIS has been recognized 
as a Food Safety Auditing Agency as per Food 
Safety and Standards (Food Safety Auditing) 
Regulations, 2018, for the following scopes:  
a) Food Processing Dairy and other sectors;  
b) Food Storage/Warehouse/Cold Storage.
As on 31 March, 2019, a total of 1,284 operative licences 
exist under the 13 management systems certification 
schemes being operated by BIS.
MAnAGEMEnT SYSTEMS  
CERTIfICATIOn SERVICES (MSCS)
In order to support the activities of conformity 
assessment schemes, BIS has established 
eight laboratories in the country to cater to 
the testing needs of samples generated from 
conformity assessment schemes, beginning with 
the establishment of the Central Laboratory at 
Sahibabad in 1962. Subsequently, four regional 
laboratories at Mohali, Kolkata, Mumbai and 
Chennai and three branch office laboratories at 
Patna, Bengaluru, and Guwahati were established. 
BIS laboratories have facilities for testing of 
products in the fields of chemical, microbiological, 
electrical and mechanical disciplines.  
In order to ensure that BIS laboratories 
follow internationally accepted practices, the 
laboratories at Mumbai, Kolkata, Chennai, 
Mohali, and Sahibabad have been accredited 
by the National Accreditation Board for Testing 
and Calibration Laboratories (NABL) as per the 
international standard IS/ISO/IEC 17025.
BIS labs have complete test facilities for 
398 Indian Standards and, in addition, partial 
test facilities are also available for 320 Indian 
Standards. During the year, the BIS laboratories 
have tested 19,532 samples of various products 
covered under Certification. In addition, the 
Gold Referral Assaying Laboratory at Chennai 
and Sahibabad have issued 5,139 test reports 
during the year. During 2018-19 under the 
modernization project, many new testing facilities 
were created or existing facilities were upgraded 
in BIS laboratories and 66 BIS officials were 
trained for IS/ISO/IEC 17025 Laboratory Quality 
Management System. During the year, 901 
samples were tested under Quality Assurance 
Activities by BIS Labs. These also include 
the samples tested during participation in a 
proficiency testing inter-lab comparison program. 
LAB RECOGNITION SCHEME
As the volume of work for testing of samples 
generated from conformity assessment schemes 
is much larger than the available capacity 
in BIS labs, BIS has established Laboratory 
Recognition Scheme (LRS) for recognition of 
Outside Laboratories (OSLs). The scheme is 
based on International Standard IS/ISO/IEC 
17025, and in line with the criteria adopted by 
the National Accreditation Board for Testing and 
Calibration Laboratories (NABL) for accreditation 
of laboratories. 
During 2018-19, 35 new labs were recognized 
and eight labs were de-recognized. As on 31 
March, 2019, the total no. of operative BIS-
recognized labs were 237, which include reputed 
R&D organizations, technical institutions, 
Government labs and private sector labs 
(Government labs–76 and Private labs-161). 
Besides this, services of 253 Government 
laboratories of specialized nature are utilized 
for different products, as and when required. To 
support the newly launched BIS Compulsory 
Registration Scheme, 65 outside labs are testing 
IT products, which were covered under Electronics 
and Information Technology Goods (Requirement 
for Compulsory Registration) Order, 2012.

INTERNATIONAL ACTIVITIES
BIS, in its capacity as the National 
Standards Body of India, is the member 
of International Organization for 
Standardization (ISO) and through the 
Indian National Committee, a member 
of International Electrotechnical 
Commission (IEC) and actively 
participates in the activities of these 
international bodies. BIS is involved in the 
development of International Standards 
as Participating (P) members or Observer 
(O) members on various Technical 
Committees and Sub-Committees, and 
has nominated technical experts in 
Working Groups of these committees. 
As on 31 March, 2019, BIS is a ‘P’ 
member in 456 Technical Committees/ 
Sub-committees of ISO and 91 Technical 
Committees/Sub-committees of IEC, 
and an ‘O’ Member in 204 Technical 
Committees/ Subcommittees of ISO and 
77 Technical Committees/Sub-committees 
of IEC. BIS holds the Secretariat of 10 
important ISO Committees dealing with 
subjects that are of interest to India. 
Chairmanship of IEC Technical Committee 
IEC TC 33 Power capacitors and their 
applications is also held by India. BIS 
also participates in various policy making 
committees (CASCO, COPOLCO and 
DEVCO) of ISO. India is currently a member 
of the ISO Council, represented by DG, BIS, 
the apex policy committee of ISO (Term 
2017-19) and a member of ISO Council 
Standing Committee on Finance (CSC/FIN) 
and ISO Council Standing Committee on 
Oversight (CSN/OVE) for term 2018-19. 
DG BIS is convenor of the ISO DEVCO/WG 
2 "Resources to enhance NSB Capabilities 
in standard development and use". India is 
also a member in Standards Management 
Board (SMB) of IEC.  
Secretariat of ISO/TC 146/SC 1 Air 
Quality – Stationary Source Emissions’ 
was handed over to BIS in Oct 2018. 
Convenorship of 19 ISO working 
groups is presently with India, of which 
ISO/IEC JTC 1/SC 7/SG 1 “Investigation of 
standards in quantum computing”, ISO/ IEC 
JTC 1/SC 7/ SG 2 “Investigation of newer 
ways to produce standards” and ISO/ IEC 
JTC 1/ SC 7/ SWG 1 “JTC 1/ SC 7 Business 
Planning Group (BPG)” were taken up 
during this period. Similarly, India holds 
convenorship of eight working groups/task 
groups/management team of IEC of which 
five were taken up during that period.
 India has project leaderships 
in areas such as water soluble tea, 
coffee, IT-enabled services – business 
process outsourcing, software quality 
assurance, software life cycle process, 
ductile iron pipes and fittings (external 
corrosion protection and construction 
and installation), air quality, leather 
identification and characterization, 
aggregate testing and concrete testing, 
rubber and plastics hoses and hose 
assemblies, aviation fuels — estimation 
of net specific energy, cellular plastics 
— determination of horizontal burning 
characteristics, etc. During this period,

  BIS has entered into MoUs and Bilateral

National Standards Bodies of various 
countries for cooperation in the areas of 
standardization, conformity assessment 
and training/capacity building and for 
mutual acceptance of each other’s 
conformity assessment results, 
respectively. BIS has so far signed 30 
MoUs and seven BCAs.

WTO-TBT Enquiry point and Trade Related 
Activities
    BIS continued its activities as the WTO/

Sponsorship of Identifi cation Numbers
  Issuer Identiﬁ cation Number (IIN)
BIS continued facilitating the issue of IINs as per 
ISO/IEC 7812 ‘Identifi cation Cards-Identifi cation 
of Issuers’ by sponsoring applications of Banks/
Financial Organizations to the American 
Bankers Association (ABA). The ISO/IEC 
7812 specifi es a numbering system for the 
identifi cation of issuers of the identifi cation 
cards used in international and/or inter-industry 
interchange. 
  World Manufacturer Identiﬁ er 
(WMI) Number
In coordination with the Society of Automotive 
Engineers (SAE), USA; BIS issues WMI Codes 
as per ISO 3780:2009 ‘Road Vehicles - World 
Manufacturer Identifi er (WMI) Code’ to 
automobile manufacturers and exporters in 
India. 143 WMI Codes were allotted to various 
vehicle manufacturers during the period. 
TECHnICAl InfORMATIOn SERVICES
National Institute of Training for 
Standardization (NITS), the training arm of 
Bureau of Indian Standards is located at 
Sector-62, Noida. The training institute is 
equipped with four well-developed training 
halls, an auditorium, library, and a hostel 
having 48 air-conditioned rooms, a dining hall, 
recreation hall and an air-conditioned gym.
In addition to the training to industry and 
BIS offi cials, three International Training 
Programmes for developing countries are 
also conducted at NITS under the ITEC 
scheme of MEA, Government of India. 
During the period 1 April, 2018 – 31 March 
2019, the National Institute of Training 
for Standardization (NITS) organized 54 
off-campus programmes and 77 open 
on-campus programmes, including two 
Consumer awareness programmes for 
2,720 participants from Industry and 
Consumer Protection Organizations. Also, 
four programmes for technical committee 
members, a programme on Solid and 
Liquid Waste Management and 22 training 
programmes were conducted for Industries:
NEW pROGRAMMES: Training on Immediate 
Life Support by First Responder; Awareness 
Training Programme on Solid and Liquid 
Waste Management; Why Quality Initiatives 
Fail; Training Programme on Road Traffi c 
Safety Management Systems; Management 
Systems for Educational Organizations; 
Training Programme on Skill Enhancement 
including soft skills; Integrated Training 
Programme on Measurement Uncertainty 
& Inter Lab Comparison-Profi ciency Testing; 
Training Programme on Managing Change; 
Orientation Programme for Diploma & 
Science Graduates at NITS, Noida and 
Orientation Training Programme for young 
Professionals.
BIS, by way of its training arm, imparts training to various 
stakeholders in the fi eld of standardization, quality and 
conformity assessment. Through these programmes, 
NITS intends to play a signifi cant role in national 
development effort by imparting knowledge, training and 
capacity building, creation of intellectual property and 
developing a culture of quality in the country.

BIS organizes Educational Utilization 
of Standards Programmes (EUS) for

colleges, etc., to inculcate in young 
minds the concepts and benefi ts of 
standardization. During the year, BIS has 
organized 40 EUS Programmes.
WORLD STANDARDS DAY:
The World Standards Day theme for the 
year 2018 was ‘International Standards 
and Fourth Industrial Revolution’. The 
day was observed across BIS ROs and

puBLIC GRIEVANCES:
Consumers can lodge their grievances 
relating to BIS certifi ed products through 
offl ine and online methods. Complaints 
are monitored regularly for redressal. 
During the year, 340 complaints were 
received and 306 complaints were 
redressed.
CITIZEN CHARTER:
Citizen's Charter has been implemented 
and timelines are being monitored every 
month through Management Control 
Reports.
ENfORCEMENT:
During 2018-19, BIS carried out 125 
search and seizures all over the country 
on fi rms misusing the ISI mark, and 
such products were seized. Efforts were 
made for timely launching of prosecution 
against the offenders in the court of law. 
BIS also issued press releases regarding 
the enforcement raids for giving wide 
publicity with the intention to create 
awareness among the consumers. 
Seventy two industry awareness programmes were 
organized during 2018-19 consisting of lecture and 
discussion sessions. Standards related to specifi c 
industrial sectors, depending on the concentration of 
industries in that area, were also highlighted. 
COnSUMER AffAIRS
SALE Of STANDARDS:
bis sells Indian Standards and Special 
Publications through 25 different sales outlets 
located at its HQ, and regional and branch 
offi ces. Sale is also done through registered 
booksellers spread across major cities of India. 
BIS also sells foreign standards (ISO, IEC, BSI 
London, DIN Germany, JIS Japan) in India 
from BIS Headquarters. BIS also sells Indian 
Standards and Special Publications through 
its e-portal, www.standardsbis.in. The Indian 
Standards are available as soft copies and 
through annual subscriptions on DVDs/online. 
A touchscreen kiosk, connected to the BIS 
e-portal, is available at the Sales Department 
of HQ to help customers browse details of 
standards, amendments and prices.
puBLICITY:
BIS creates awareness about its various 
activities through print, electronic, outdoor 
and digital media. In 2018-19, publicity 
campaigns were executed mainly to 
promote importance of ISI-mark, hallmark 
on gold jewellery, and compulsory 
registration schemes. BIS participated in 
popular consumer and industrial fairs/
exhibitions to disseminate information 
about its activities. BIS is also active on 
all popular social media platforms and 
regularly posts regarding its activities and 
various events.    
VIGILANCE ACTIVITIES:
The Vigilance Department functions in 
close coordination with Central Vigilance 
Commission (CVC), Central Bureau of 
Investigation (CBI) and Ministry of Consumer 
Affairs, Food & Public Distribution. The 
activities, inter alia, include:
l  Preventive Vigilance (for example, 
streamlining of procedures, training, 
preparation of Agreed List and List of Offi cers 
of doubtful integrity).
l  Punitive Vigilance (for example, scrutiny of 
complaints received, investigations, disciplinary 
action against the offi cers at fault). 
l  Surveillance and Detection (vigilance
audits, scrutiny of annual property returns, 
monitoring, review meetings).
BIS celebrated Vigilance Awareness Week 
from 29 October, 2018, to 3 November, 2018. 
The week-long event had many activities 
such as display of banners, distribution 
of pamphlets, and quiz, essay and slogan 
writing competitions on the theme: Eradicate 
Corruption – Build a New India. Workshops 
on preventive vigilance were organized in 
Northern Regional Offi ce, Central Laboratory 
including Ghaziabad Branch Offi ce and 
Bangalore Branch Offi ce. Preventive 
vigilance audits of Haryana Branch Offi ce, 
Bhubaneswar Branch Offi ce, Central 
Laboratory and Bangalore Branch Offi ce 
were carried out during the year.

WiTh continued emphasis on digitization, 
efforts are being made to make optimal 
use of Information Technology (IT) in day-
to-day activities of BIS. The progress made 
during the year is given below:
INTEGRATED BIS WEB pORTAL
An integrated web portal is in operation 
since 15 May, 2017, which automates 
the major certification activities of BIS. 
This portal enables applicants/licencees 
to apply for a new licence online. It also 
enables renewal, inclusion, surveillance, 
payment of fees, and other necessary 
actions on existing licenses online. 
HuMAN RESOuRCE AND fINANCE 
MANAGEMENT SYSTEM (HfMS)
A customizable HFMS software for 
managing support services of BIS (i.e., 
Accounts & Finance, Human Resource, 
Administration, Establishment, etc.) 
has been procured from CDAC, Noida, and 
the same is under customization to suit 
BIS requirements. 
SOfTWARE fOR STANDARDIZATION 
A software is in operation that automates 
all the stages involved in the formulation 
of standards and their amendments. A 
user-friendly “standard locator” is also 
available on the BIS website to help 
outside users search for relevant Indian 
Standards. Portals are also operational 
for online sale and subscription of Indian 
Standards. 
CRS pORTAL
A separate web portal (www.crsbis.in) 
is in operation since June 1, 2014, to 
operate the Compulsory Registration 
Scheme for Electronics and IT goods. The 
portal www.crsbis.in facilitates online 
submission of applications for grant of 
registration by applicants, renewal, model 
inclusion, model deletion, etc., for the 
existing registrations and online payment 
of fees. 
NATIONAL LABORATORY DIRECTORY
A National Laboratory Directory has been 
created in-house in order to prepare a 
comprehensive database of laboratories 
in the country that have test facilities 
for the products. This can be utilized by 
various stakeholders for identifying the 
laboratories of their interest. 
MANAGING ONLINE COMpLAINTS
A software is in operation to facilitate 
outside users to e-register complaints, 
track their status and view details. The 
complaints are monitored and redressed.
WTO-TBT MODuLE
A module is operational to host WTO-TBT 
notifications. It enables outside users to 
register themselves to receive notifications 
on their email IDs.
A Unified Threat Management (UTM) device has been 
installed at the BIS HQ to streamline the management 
of network and Internet bandwidth in BIS. The portals 
for electronic sale of standards, compulsory registration 
scheme and production servers installed in the 
headquarters have also been SSL(Secure Sockets Layer) 
enabled for increased security. 
InfORMATIOn TECHnOlOGY SERVICES
 ITp pORTAL
A portal is in operation for International 
Training Programme participants/alumni. 
This portal facilitates ITP participants/
alumni to register themselves, view 
details of other alumni, view/comment/
reply on discussion forums, etc. 
BIS WEBSITE
The revamped website of BIS, www.bis.
gov.in, was developed in compliance with 
the Guidelines for Indian Government 
Websites (GIGW). It is now more 
informative and interactive. 
BIS INTRANET 
A module is operational in BIS for 
dissemination of information through 
circulars/orders/guidelines, etc. which 
are uploaded on the BIS Intranet. 
pAYROLL MANAGEMENT SOfTWARE
A payroll management software is 
operational at BIS HQ to process salaries, 
pensions, general provident fund, income 
tax, and others. 
LIBRARY MANAGEMENT SYSTEM
A library management system is 
operational, which acts as a repository 
of the books, publications and journals 
available at Library, BIS HQ. The 
software being managed by Library at 
BIS HQ, is accessible to BIS employees.
 
VIDEO CONfERENCING
A cloud-based Video Conferencing (VC) 
system is regularly being utilized by BIS 
officers for meetings, interaction with 
national and international stakeholders.
E-pROCuREMENT
E-procurement is operational in BIS offices 
through the Central Public Procurement 
Portal (CPPP) of National Informatics 
Centre (NIC). The procurement of goods 
and services available on GeM is being 
made through it in BIS. 
IT INfRASTRuCTuRE

strengthen the bandwidth, and further 
enhancement to 200 Mbps is in process.

rules/policies have been updated to

EASE Of DOING BuSINESS

LIBRARY SERVICES
Located at headquarters, the BIS Technical 
Library is a National Resources Centre for 
information on standards and related matters 
and serves industry, trade, government, 
researchers and consumers. It is the largest 
library of standards in the South Asian Region, 
with a floor area of 1,000 square meters. The 
collection includes about 4 lakh standards 
from the world and more than 72,000 
technical books. In 2018-19, close to 2,547 
visitors were provided four exhaustive subject 
bibliographies and reference materials. It 
assisted trade and industries by answering 
2,100 queries. The library regularly updates 
mechanised database of publications 
received. Under the Annual Action Plan, 
66,622 numbers of publications have been 
electronically indexed during 2018-19. 
SEXuAL HARASSMENT Of WOMEN 
AT THE WORkpLACE
In compliance with the guidelines of the 
Supreme Court of India on the prevention of 
sexual harassment of women at the work 
place, an Internal Complaints Committee 
was constituted in the Bureau of Indian 
Standards in February 1998 that has since 
been reconstituted vide OM No. Estt-III/
ICC/76:01/2018 dated 9 January, 2018. 
The composition of the constituted Internal 
Complaints Committee (ICC) at BIS, HQ, 
New Delhi, consists of a senior level woman 
employee as Presiding Officer along with 
two other members from the HQ and one 
member from the All-India Democratic 
Women’s Association (AIDWA), New Delhi. 
Similarly, ICC has been constituted in each 
of our regional offices at Mumbai, Chennai, 
Kolkata, and Chandigarh separately. The ICC 
-HQ celebrated International Women’s Day at 
BIS, HQ, New Delhi on 8 March, 2019, with the 
participation of women employees posted at 
all the BIS Offices in the NCR, i.e., Faridabad, 
Ghaziabad, Central Laboratory, NITS, Noida, 
and other BIS offices. 
SCHEMES fOR pERSONS 
WITH DISABILITIES
1) As per the Government of India (GOI) 
instructions, 4% reservation is being provided 
to persons with disabilities in direct recruitment 
under Group A, B, C & D posts. 
2) BIS also allows 12 casual leaves to the 
physically challenged/disabled people 
instead of eight casual leaves permissible to a 
regular employee.
3) Physically challenged/differently-abled 
people are paid transport allowance at double 
the rates prescribed for other employees.

l A total of 881 Indian Standards covering 
a wide range of subjects were formulated 
during the year. As on 31 March, 2019, 
20,227 standards were in force. Some of 
the important Standards formulated during 
the period include those on Vermicompost, 
Galvanized Structural Steel, RFID System 
for Automotive Application, Bullet 
Resistant Jacket, Aviation Turbine Fuel Jet, 
Polycarbonate Protective Shields, etc.
l To create awareness and disseminate 
information on standards and to facilitate 
their implementation, BIS organizes 
seminars and workshops for stakeholders. 
The notable events were on National 
Building Code of India 2016, National 
Seminar on “Smart Machining and Industry 
4.0” and BIS-IEC International Conference 
on Smart City Standardization.
l BIS, during the year, increased 
its involvement and participation in 
international standardization committees 
of International Organization for 
Standardization (ISO) and International 
Electrotechnical Commission (IEC).  
A total of 5,812 Indian Standards 
have been so far harmonized with the 
International Standards. 
l BIS signed cooperation agreements 
with National Standards Body of other 
countries like the Hellenic Organization for 
Standardization (NQIS/ELOT), Greece, and 
Ghana Standards Authority (GSA), Ghana, 
and the Agency for Standardization, 
Metrology and Certification of Uzbekistan. 
l A four-member Indian delegation led 
by Secretary, Department of Consumer 
Affairs, along with the DG BIS attended 
the ISO General Assembly (GA) and 
related meetings at Geneva, Switzerland, 
during 24-28 September, 2018. During 
ISO GA 2018, meetings of ISO Council, 
DEVCO (ISO Committee on Developing 
Country Matters) and General Assembly 
were attended. DG BIS was a panelist 
in the TMB-DEVCO panel discussion 
on "Mechanism to facilitate developing 
country participation in technical work."  
l Conformity assessment has been a 
leading activity of BIS. This includes 
product certification through the BIS 
Product Certification Marks Scheme (ISI 
Mark), Foreign Manufacturers Certification 
Scheme and Compulsory Registration 
Scheme, Hallmarking, Management 
System Certifications and of testing 
of product samples in laboratories for 
the purpose of certification. In line with 
the Government policy of ease of doing 
business, BIS introduced simplified 
procedures of certification and online 
registration processes for its registration 
schemes. 18 products were covered 
for the first time under the product 
certification scheme. 
l 19,532 samples were tested by 
BIS Laboratories. In addition, 5,139 
samples were tested by Gold Referral 
Assaying Laboratory in Chennai and 
Sahibabad. As on March 2019, 237 
outside labs stood recognized by BIS, 
including 65 labs for testing of IT 
products, which were covered under 
Electronics and Information Technology 
Goods (Requirement for Compulsory 
Registration) Order, 2012. 
ACHIEVEMEnTS Of 2018-19
In 2018-19, 4,082 product certification licences were 
granted, taking the count of operative licences to 34,414. 
Under FMCS, 136 licenses were issued, raising the 
number of licences to 874 against 100 Indian Standards 
even as 49 electronics and IT goods were notified under 
the CRS, taking the registrations to 17,251.
l As on 31 March, 2019, the number of operative 
licences for hallmarking of gold and silver 
jewellery was 24,623 and 2,065, respectively. The 
number of operative BIS recognized assaying and 
hallmarking centres was 797. During the year 
about 4.49 crore articles of jewellery/artefacts 
were hallmarked. 
l BIS has been provisionally recognized by Food 
and Safety Auditing Agency under the draft Food 
Safety and Standards (Food Safety Auditing) 
regulations, 2018, for the Dairy sector.
l 797 Quality Management System (QMS) 
Certification licences, 189 Environmental 
Management Systems (EMS) Certification 
licences, 111 Occupational Health & Safety 
Management Systems (OHSMS) Certification 
licences, 22 Food Safety Management System 
(FSMS) Certification licences, 108 Service Quality 
Management System (SQMS) Certification 
licences, 28 Energy Management System (EnMS) 
Certification licences, 01 Hazards Analysis and 
Critical Control Point (HACCP) stand-alone 
licences, 24 HACCP integrated with QMS licences 
and one Medical Devices Management System 
(MDMS) licence were operative as on  
31 March, 2019.
OTHER ACHIEVEMENTS 
l In an endeavour to build the capacity of the 
developing world in the fields of standards and 
conformity assessment, the 15th International 
Training Programme on Management Systems, 
the 51st International Training Programme on 
Standardization and Quality Assurance and 
the 9th International Training Programme on 
Laboratory Quality Management Systems (LQMS) 
were organized by National Institute of Training 
for Standardization (NITS) of BIS during the year. 
These training programmes were sponsored by 
the Ministry of External Affairs and attended by 
97 participants from several developing countries.
l During the year, 182 consumer awareness 
programmes, 72 industry awareness programmes 
and 40 programmes for educational utilization 
of standards were organized. Three hundred 
and forty complaints were received and 306 
complaints were redressed during the year. 
l BIS carried out 125 search and seizures all over 
the country on firms misusing the ISI mark and 
products were also seized. Efforts were made 
for timely launching of prosecution against the 
offenders in the court of law.
l As a green initiative, Rooftop Solar Power 
Plants are installed in HQ, New Delhi (100 kW), 
SRO Lab, Chennai (100 kW), WRO, Mumbai 
(40 kW), and NITS, Noida (50 kW), Hyderabad 
Branch Office (40 kW), Eastern Regional Office, 
Kolkata (24.4 kW), and BIS Eastern Regional 
Office Laboratory, Kolkata (40 kW). BIS has also 
implemented Net Metering at HQ, New Delhi, 
NITS, Noida, and Hyderabad Branch Office.
l As on 31 March, 2019, a total of 1,284 
employees were on the rolls of BIS.
l Vigilance Awareness Week was celebrated in 
BIS from 29 October, 2018, to 3 November, 2018.
l Hindi Pakhwara was celebrated from 1-14 
September, 2018, at BIS HQ in which seven 
competitions related to Hindi were organized 
and 42 prizes were awarded to the successful 
participants.
l BIS recorded a total income (excluding 
interest from investment) of about `705.79 
crore, compared to `587.36 crore during the 
previous year (2017-18), an increase of 20.16%. 
BIS continued to be self-sufficient in meeting 
its expenditure and other liabilities from its own 
income for the 30th consecutive year.

For the 30th consecutive year, i.e. 2018-19, Bureau of Indian Standards  continued 
to be self-reliant in meeting its expenditure and other liabilities. The total income 
(excluding income from investment) during the year 2018-19   amounted to  
`70579.52  lakh as against  `58736.57 lakh in the previous year, resulting in an 
increase of  20.16%. The largest contribution to the income was from Product 
Certification Marking Fee, which stood at  `55410.11 lakh against  `49875.82 lakh 
in the previous year, i.e., an increase of  11.10%. The total revenue expenditure 
during the year 2018-19 amounted to  `27901.09 lakh as against  `27168.34 lakh 
in 2017-18, registering an increase of 2.70%.

--- Page 67 ---
58   i bis  l  annual report 2018-19
6.2 
All Government Grants/Assistance from Ministries and their utilization are 
shown in the Earmarked/Endowment Fund Schedule.    
6.3 
The Government Grants/Assistance utilized towards Capital Cost of setting  
of projects and acquisition of Fixed Assets are shown as addition to  
Corpus/Capital Fund.
7. 
fOReiGN CURReNCY tRANsACtiONs 
7.1 
Transactions denominated in Foreign Currency are accounted at the exchange 
rate prevailing at the date of the transaction.
7.2 
Current Liabilities are converted at the exchange rate prevailing at the  
end of the year and the relevant gain/loss is transferred to Income & 
Expenditure Account.
8. 
pAY & AllOWANCes 
 
The payments of Pay & Allowances and leave encashment are charged to 
Income & Expenditure Account on cash basis under Pay and Allowances.
9. 
RetiReMeNt beNefits
9.1 
Liability towards pension of retired employees and pension and gratuity of 
existing employees for past service based on the Actuarial Valuation is accrued 
and provided in the Pension/Gratuity Liability Fund Account shown under the 
Schedule - Earmarked/Endowment Fund.  
9.2 
Based on the Actuarial Valuation Report, Annual Contribution to the Fund is 
provided in the Income & Expenditure Account with corresponding credit to 
Pension/Gratuity Liability Fund Account.  
9.3 
The actual payments of all pensionary benefits and recurring pension during 
the year are debited to Pension/Gratuity Liability Fund Account. 
10. 
lOANs tO eMplOYees
 
The Interest on House Building, Conveyance and Computer Loan given to 
employees is accounted on cash basis after the recovery of the principal 
amount of Loan.
11. 
Gpf ACCOUNts
 
The surplus/deficit in the GPF Account of employees is treated as  
income/expense of the Bureau.
1. 
ACCCOUNtiNG CONVeNtiON
 
The Financial Statements are prepared on the basis of historical cost 
convention, unless otherwise stated and generally on the accrual method of 
accounting, except the Certification Income and the interest due on default 
investments, which are accounted on cash basis.
2. 
iNVeNtORies
 
The value of Stock of Indian Standards and other publications are not accounted 
for as a matter of policy. However, the Stock of paper, laboratory consumables, 
spares, stationery and gold are valued at cost.
3. 
iNVestMeNt
3.1 
The Investments are usually carried at cost.  
3.2 
The premium paid on acquisition of permanent investment is amortized on a 
time proportion basis upto the date of maturity. 
4. 
fiXeD Asset
4.1 
Fixed Assets are stated at Cost of acquisition inclusive of inward Freight, Duties 
and Taxes.
4.2 
Fixed Assets acquired out of Grants/Assistance from Ministries are capitalized 
at values stated, by corresponding credit to Corpus/Capital Fund.
4.3 
Fixed Assets received by way of non-monetary grants are capitalized at values 
stated by corresponding credit to Corpus/Capital Fund.
5. 
DepReCiAtiON
 
Depreciation is provided on written down value method as per the rates 
specified in the Income Tax Act 1961.
6. 
GOVeRNMeNt GRANts/AssistANCe
6.1 
Government Grants/Assistance are accounted on realization basis.

sCHeDUle 17 
CONTINGENT LIABILITIES AND  
NOTES ON ACCOUNTS 
1         CONtiNGeNt liAbilities
1.1 
Disputed Demands of Service Tax (excluding penalty and interest):- These 
disputed demands relate to service tax on revenue sharing receipts from  
Hallmarking & Assaying Centres, payment of membership fee to ISO/IEC, library 
membership fee and service-tax liability under reverse charge mechanism which 
have been contested by BIS at the following offices:
1.2 
NBCC, the consultant for the Jaipur Building and Training Institute Building 
NOIDA:- NBCC has claimed payment of `27.60 lakh and `17.04 lakh for the  
works carried out at Jaipur and NITS Noida Buildings, respectively. However, 
physical verification of the work done by the contractor(s) is not yet completed 
as some corrective actions are yet to be taken by NBCC and the settlement 
of accounts with them is under progress. As the amount payable is subject to 
physical verification as per the contract, therefore, these claims have not been 
taken as Addition to Assets and Liabilities as on 31.03.2019. It had been decided 
by EC that no payment shall be released to NBCC against these two projects till 
settlement of the issues in the New Central AC Plant at Headquarter. 
1.3 
Suit No. 3016 of 2010 in Hon’ble Bombay High Court:- Contingent Liability  
of `73,82.90 lakh along with interest may arise due to damages claimed by  
M/s. National Food Products (India) Pvt. Ltd. from BIS, for the alleged loss 
suffered by them because of the delay in renewal of their licence for Packaged  
Drinking Water.
1.4 
Bank Guarantee: Bank Guarantee of  `5.00 lakh valid upto 05.01.2020 was  
issued by Syndicate Bank in favour of Director General of Disasters,  
Response & Fire Services, Hyderabad.

year 2018-19 has been carried to Capital/Corpus Fund of BIS.
2.3 
National Pension Scheme Fund:- The National Pension Scheme (NPS) is applicable 
to all employees who joined BIS  after 01.01.2004. The employees’ contribution 
and BIS contribution in respect of those who are enrolled with the Regulator are 
remitted to PFDRA on monthly basis. However, the employees contribution   and 
BIS contribution in respect of employees who are yet to be enrolled with the 
Regulator is kept with BIS under NPS Fund Account and is invested by BIS. The 
interest calculated at the rate equivalent to the interest rate of GPF is credited to 
their accounts. The balance in the NPS Fund with BIS as on 31.03.2019 amounted 
to `19,95,125 {Schedule 2, column 6}.

2.4.2 BIS had made an investment of `200.00 lakh in the bonds of U.P. Cooperative 
& Spinning Mills Federation Ltd. (UPCSMFL), an undertaking of Uttar Pradesh 
Government on 17.12.1998 @ 16% per annum. UPCSMFL had defaulted in the 
payment of interest and principal on maturity dates. The maturity of principal was 
due on 30.04.2003 (33%), 30.10.2003 (33%) and 30.04.2004 (34%). The interest 
is under default since 01.05.2000 which amounts to `128.00 lakh till date of 
maturity at coupon rate. BIS had filed a case through Petition No. 451/2002 before 
the National Consumer Disputes Redressal Commission (NCDRC). The Hon’ble 
NCDRC  pronounced its judgement on 01.02.2016 and ordered the opposite party 
No. 01 (UPCSMFL) & opposite party No. 02 (Government of Uttar Pradesh, the 
guarantor) to pay a sum of `200 lakh jointly and severally to BIS along with the 
interest @ 9% only from 01.05.2000 till the date of realization. A Caveat Petition 
has also been filed by BIS in the Supreme Court on 03.03.2016.
 
The UPCSMFL filed the Review Petitions No. 48/2017 & 138/2017 praying 
the Hon’ble NCDRC to (i) allow this Review Petition and recall order dated 
01.02.2016 passed by this Hon’ble Court in Original Petition No. 451/2002, and 
(ii) set aside order 01.02.2016 passed by this Hon’ble Court in Original Petition 
No. 451/2002 and hear this case afresh. The Review Petitions were dismissed 
by Hon’ble NCDRC. The Hon’ble NCDRC has issued Dasti Notice of E.A. No. 43 of 
2016 and of all M.A.s filed therein to the Respondents/Judgement Debtors and 
to the official Liquidator. The said Executive application is listed for 04.07.2019 in 
Hon’ble NCDRC.

NBCC towards this project. The payments of `86,07,396 made upto 2008-09

of Fixed Assets [Schedule 4]. Executive Committee (EC) in its 79th meeting held

2.9 
Surplus in General Provident Fund Accounts: There was a  surplus (i.e. excess of income over 
expenditure) of `85,48,121 in BIS Employees General Provident Fund Accounts during 2018-
19.  This surplus is due to higher yield earned by BIS on investments of GPF as compared to 
the interest credited to subscriber accounts. This has been treated as income of the Bureau as 
per the Accounting Policy [Schedule 13 item (g)].
2.10 
Income-Tax Exemption:  
2.10.1 Central Board of Direct Taxes (CBDT), Department of Revenue, Ministry of Finance, Govt. of 
India, vide its Notification No. 88/2014 dated 23.12.2014 had notified BIS under Section 10 
(46) of Income-Tax Act, 1961 for the Assessment Years 2012-13 to 2016-17. The CBDT  has  
further notified BIS under this section for the Assessment Years 2017-18 to 2021-22 vide 
Notification No. 111/2016 dated 01.12.2016. As a result of this notification of CBDT, the income 
of BIS is not taxable till Assessment Year 2021-22.  
2.10.2 Income-Tax Exemption granted to BIS under section 10(23)(c)(iv), which was withdrawn by 
DG:IT(E) vide order dated 24.02.2012 from Assessment Year 2009-10 and onwards was 
restored by it vide order dated 04.12.2012 in compliance with the order of Hon’ble High Court 
of Delhi. Therefore, the tax exemption of BIS under section 10(23)(c)(iv) of Income Tax Act is 
also available. The DG IT(E) had filed SLP in Hon’ble Supreme Court under Article 136 of the 
Constitution of India against allowing the Writ Petition of BIS by the Hon’ble High Court of 
Delhi.  The SLP filed by DG:IT(E) has been converted into Civil Appeal which is pending in the 
Hon’ble Supreme Court.
2.11 
The Annual Accounts have been prepared in the Uniform Formats of Accounts prescribed by 
the Ministry of Finance.
2.12 
The previous year figures have been re-grouped wherever found necessary to make them 
comparable with current year groups and figures. 
2.13 
Figures in Final Accounts have been rounded off to the nearest rupee.

We have audited the attached Balance Sheet of Bureau of Indian Standards (BIS), New 
Delhi as at 31 March 2019, Income & Expenditure Account and Receipts & Payments 
Account for the year ended on that date under Section 19 (2) of the Comptroller & 
Auditor General’s (Duties, Powers & Conditions of Service) Act, 1971 read with Section 
24(2) of the Bureau of Indian Standards Act 2016.  These financial statements include 
the accounts of twenty four Branch Offices, four Regional Offices, Central Laboratory 
at Sahibabad and the National Institute for Training on Standardization (NITS) at 
Noida.  These financial statements are the responsibility of the BIS’s management. Our 
responsibility is to express an opinion on these financial statements based on our audit.
2. 
This Separate Audit Report (SAR) contains the comments of the Comptroller & 
Auditor General of India (CAG) on the accounting treatment only with regard to 
classification, conformity with the best accounting practices, accounting standards 
and disclosure norms, etc. Audit observations on financial transactions with regard 
to compliance with the Laws, Rules & Regulations (Propriety and Regularity) and 
efficiency-cum-performance aspects, etc., if any, are reported through Inspection 
Reports/CAG’s Audit Reports separately.
3. 
We have conducted our audit in accordance with auditing standards generally 
accepted in India. These standards require that we plan and perform the audit to 
obtain reasonable assurance about whether the financial statements are free from 
material misstatements. An audit includes examining, on a test basis, evidences 
supporting the amounts and disclosures in the financial statements. An audit 
also includes assessing the accounting principles used and significant estimates 
made by management, as well as evaluating the overall presentation of financial 
statements. We believe that our audit provides a reasonable basis for our opinion. 
4. 
Based on our audit, we report that:
 
i) We have obtained all the information and explanations, which to the best of  our 
knowledge and belief were necessary for the purpose of our audit.
 
ii) The Balance Sheet, Income & Expenditure/Receipts & Payments Account dealt 
with by this report have been drawn up in the Uniform Format of Accounts as 
prescribed by the Ministry of Finance.
 
iii) In our opinion, proper books of accounts and other relevant records have been 
maintained, under section 24(1) of The Bureau of Indian Standards Act 2016, in so 
far as it appears from our examination of such books.
 
iv) We further report that:
A. 
significant Accounting policies (schedule-16)
A.1 
As per Schedule of Significant Accounting Policies, Accounts have been prepared on 
accrual method of accounting. Further as significant accounting policy no. 8, the payment 
of Pay & Allowances and leave encashment are being maintained on cash basis. This 
Accounting Policy to the extent of maintenance of accounts of Pay & Allowances and leave 
encashment on cash basis is not in conformity with the Uniform format of accounts and 
Accounting Standard 15 issued by ICAI.

b. 
General:
B.1 
BIS has been accounting Subscription for Membership of International Organization 
for Standardization (ISO) and to Membership of International Electrotechnical 
Commission (IEC) on cash basis instead of accrual basis in contravention of it’s 
declared accounting policy. 
B.2 
Consolidated Fixed Assets Register has not been maintained. Fixed Assets Register 
of BIS Headquarter has not been maintained in format of GFR Rule 40. Further, Fixed 
Assets Register maintained at BIS Headquarter do not include assets like Land, 
Building and Residential flats. In addition, BIS possesses land on lease hold basis 
also; however, segregation of land on freehold and lease hold basis has not been 
depicted in Accounts. 
B.3 
As per Schedule 8 of Fixed Assets of Uniform Format of Accounts, Furniture and 
fixtures, Office equipment and Computer/peripherals were to be shown separately 
since these items have different rates of depreciation. However, in the Schedule of 
Fixed Assets, these items were clubbed and shown under single head.  As a result, 
actual amount of each item has not been reflected in Sch-4 of Fixed Assets.
C.  
Grants-in-aid
C.1 
During 2018-19, BIS received Grants-in-aid of ` 2.00 crore for setting up of 
Hallmarking Centres and for quality infrastructure for consumer protection from 
the Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food & Public Distribution.  It had `. 0.04 crore as 
interest income and unspent balance of ` 6.97 crore of the previous year.  Out of total 
available amount of ` 9.01 crore, a sum of ` 1.99 crore was utilized during the year 
leaving a balance of ` 7.02 crore at the end of the year. 
D.  
Management letter: Deficiencies which have not been included in the Audit Report 
have been brought to the notice of the Director General, Bureau of Indian Standards 
(BIS) through a management letter issued separately for remedial/ corrective action.
v. 
Subject to our observations in the preceding paragraphs, we report that the Balance 
Sheet, Income & Expenditure Account and Receipts & Payments Account dealt with by 
this report are in agreement with the books of accounts.
vi. 
In our opinion and to the best of our information and according to the explanations 
given to us, the said financial statements read together with Notes on Accounts, 
and subject to the significant matters stated above and other matters mentioned in 
Annexure to this Audit Report give a true and fair view in conformity with accounting 
principles generally accepted in India;
a. 
In so far as it relates to the Balance Sheet, of the state of affairs of the Bureau of 
Indian Standards as at 31 March 2019; and
b. 
In so far as it relates to Income and Expenditure Account of the Surplus for the year 
ended on that date.
place: New Delhi 
Date: 09.01.2020
for and on behalf of C& AG of india
Director General of Audit Central 
expeniture
1. 
Adequacy of internal audit system
 
Internal audit has been conducted by Chartered Accountant Firm up to 
March 2019, however, Internal Audit Report was not produced to audit. 
2. 
Adequacy of internal Control system
(i) 
Fixed Assets register was not properly maintained. Register of Valuables 
was not maintained by Western Regional Office of BIS.
(ii) 
Sundry creditors towards TA/DA of ` 50.68 lakh pertaining to the period 
2007-08 to 2018-19 were outstanding as of March 2019. 
(iii) 
Sundry Debtors for certification amounting to ` 29.17 lakh pertaining to 
the period prior to 2015-16 to 2018-19 were outstanding as on 31.3.2018.
(iv) 
Advances amounting to ` 6.76 crore for the period 2006-07 to 2016-17 
were outstanding as on 31.3.2019.
(v) 
25 Paras of Inspection Report for the period 2002-03 to 2014-17 were 
outstanding. 
(vi) 
Accounts Recoverable (Others) amounting to ` 2.87 crore were 
outstanding as on 31.3.2019.  Out of which, an amount of ` 54.13 lakh 
were pertained to the period 2006-2007 to 2016-17. 
 
In view of the above, the internal control system of BIS needs to  
be strengthened.
3. 
system of physical verification of assets
(i) 
The physical verification of fixed assets of BIS Headquarters, New Delhi 
and WRO, Mumbai has been conducted up to March 2019 and no material 
deficiency reported.
4. 
system of physical verification of inventory
(i) 
Physical verification of Stationery and Consumables has been conducted 
only upto March 2018.
(ii) 
Physical verification of Books and Publication had been conducted upto 
2018-19.
5. 
Regularity in payment of dues.
 
No Payment over six month in respect of statutory dues were outstanding 
as on 31.03.2019.
